Thus by construction, the triplet in $\mathcal{R}$ is of the form $((\text{env}_{V},\Delta);s;\bot,\Delta_{i},s_{i})$, and we know that $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vdash\text{env}_{V}$.
The transition
\begin{equation*}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}\vDash_{s_{i}}\left<(\text{env}_{V},\Delta);s;\bot,\text{env}_{TS},\text{env}_{V}^{\prime}\right>\rightarrow\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s_{i}}\left<s;\bot,\text{env}_{TSV}\right>\end{equation*}\par\noindent can therefore be concluded by rule \nameref{stm_rt_return}, for any appropriately shaped $\text{env}_{S},\text{env}_{V}^{\prime}$, and $(s;\bot,\Delta,s_{i})\in\mathcal{R}$ by construction.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@If $q_{i}^{\prime}$ is of the form $\texttt{throw};q_{i}^{\prime\prime}$, then this satisfies Case~\ref{case:typi1} of Definition~\ref{def:typing_interpretation_stm}.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@If $q_{i}^{\prime}$ is non-empty and not of the form $\texttt{throw};q_{i}^{\prime\prime}$, then $(q_{i}^{\prime};\bot,\Delta_{i}^{\prime},s_{i}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}^{i}$, $\text{{fin}}(q_{i}^{\prime};\bot,\Delta_{i}^{\prime},s_{i}^{\prime})=(\Delta_{i},s_{i})$, and there exists at least one transition
\begin{equation*}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}^{\prime}\vDash_{s_{i}^{\prime}}\left<q_{i}^{\prime};\bot,\text{env}_{TSV}\right>\rightarrow\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}^{\prime\prime}\vDash_{s_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\left<q_{i}^{\prime\prime};\bot,\text{env}_{T};\text{env}_{SV}^{\prime}\right>\end{equation*}\par\noindent for all appropriately shaped $\text{env}_{SV}$ built from $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}^{\prime}$ according to Definition~\ref{def:construction_envsv}, with $(q_{i}^{\prime\prime};\bot,\Delta_{i}^{\prime\prime},s_{i}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}^{i}$.
There are now two possibilities, depending on $q_{i}^{\prime\prime}$:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@If $q_{i}^{\prime\prime}$ is empty, the reduct is of the form $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}\vDash_{s_{i}}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T};\text{env}_{SV}^{\prime}\right>$.
Then, the transition
\@@amsalign
\par\noindent can likewise be concluded, and $((\text{env}_{V},\Delta);s;\bot,\Delta_{i},s_{i})\in\mathcal{R}$ by construction.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@Otherwise, if $q_{i}^{\prime\prime}$ is non-empty, then the reduct is instead of the form $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}^{\prime\prime}\vDash_{s_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\left<q_{i}^{\prime\prime};\bot,\text{env}_{T};\text{env}_{SV}^{\prime}\right>$ and $(q_{i}^{\prime\prime};\bot,\Delta_{i}^{\prime\prime},s_{i}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}^{i}$.
Then, the transition
\@@amsalign
\par\noindent can likewise be concluded.
Finally, by Theorem~\ref{theorem:stacks_term_level}, we have that $\text{{fin}}(q_{i}^{\prime\prime};\bot,\Delta_{i}^{\prime\prime},s_{i}^{\prime\prime})=(\Delta_{i},s_{i})$, so by construction we also have that $(q_{i}^{\prime\prime};(\text{env}_{V},\Delta);s;\bot,\Delta_{i}^{\prime\prime},s_{i}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{R}$.
\qed\end{itemize}\end{itemize}\end@proof\par Beforewecanshowthelemmafordelegatecalls(Lemma~\ref{lemma:compatibility_stm_dcall}),weneedafewauxiliaryresults,whichensurethatchangingthetypesofthetwomagicvariables\texttt{this}and\texttt{value}doesnotpermitanyunwantedinformationflows.Inthefollowingthreelemmasbelow(Lemmas~\ref{lemma:dcall_exp_trans}--\ref{lemma:dcall_stm_type})weusethefollowingnotations.Let\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$\Delta_{1}\triangleq\Delta,\texttt{this}:\text{var}(I_{1},s_{1}),\texttt{value}:\text{var}(\text{int},s_{1}^{\prime})$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Delta_{2}\triangleq\Delta,\texttt{this}:\text{var}(I_{2},s_{2}),\texttt{value}:\text{var}(\text{int},s_{2}^{\prime})$,
\end{itemize}\par\noindent where$\Sigma\vdash(I_2, s_2) \mathrel{<:}(I_1, s_1)$and$s_2' \sqsubseteq s_1'$,and\begin{equation*}\forall q\in(\dom\left(\Gamma(I^{Y})\right)\cap(\mathcal{N}_{F}\cup\{\texttt{balance}\})).\Gamma(I_{1})(q)=\Gamma(I_{2})(q),\end{equation*}\par\noindent Furthermore,let$$\text{env}_{SV}$^1$bebuiltfrom$\Gamma, \Delta_1$and$$\text{env}_{SV}$^2$bebuiltfrom$\Gamma, \Delta_2$accordingtoDefinition~\ref{def:construction_envsv}.\par\begin{lemma}If $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{1}\vDash_{B_{s}}\left<e,\text{env}_{SV}^{1}\right>\rightarrow v_{1}$,
then $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{2}\vDash_{B_{s}}\left<e,\text{env}_{SV}^{2}\right>\rightarrow v_{2}$.
\end{lemma}\par\@proof Byinductiononthederivationofthetransition.Forthebasestep,thecasefor\nameref{exp_rt_val}isimmediateandthecasefor\nameref{exp_rt_var}isimmediateaswell,exceptinthetwospecialcaseswhere$x = {this}$or$x = {value}$;then,subtypingisusedtocoercethetypeuptomatch$B_s$.Fortheinductivestep,wereasononthelastruleused.When\nameref{exp_rt_op}isthelastrule,weconcludeby$n$applicationsoftheinductionhypothesis(oneforeachoftheoperands$e_1, \ldots, e_n$).When\nameref{exp_rt_field}isthelastrule,then$e = e'.p$;weapplytheinductionhypothesisto$e'$andconcludeby\nameref{exp_rt_field}.\qed\end@proof\par\par\begin{lemma}If $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{1}\vDash_{s}\left<Q,\text{env}_{T};\text{env}_{SV}^{1}\right>\rightarrow\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{1}^{\prime}\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{T};\text{env}_{SV}^{1^{\prime}}\right>$,
then $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{2}\vDash_{s}\left<Q,\text{env}_{T};\text{env}_{SV}^{2}\right>\rightarrow\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{2}^{\prime}\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{T};\text{env}_{SV}^{2^{\prime}}\right>$
\end{lemma}\par\@proof Byacaseanalysisoftheruleusedtoconcludethetransition.\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@For \nameref{stm_rt_skip}, \nameref{stm_rt_whilefalse}, \nameref{stm_rt_delv}, \nameref{stm_rt_return}, and \nameref{stm_rt_restore} the result is immediate.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@For \nameref{stm_rt_if}, \nameref{stm_rt_whiletrue}, \nameref{stm_rt_decv}, \nameref{stm_rt_assv}, \nameref{stm_rt_call}, \nameref{stm_rt_dcall} and \nameref{stm_rt_fcall}, we apply Lemma~\ref{lemma:dcall_exp_trans} for the expression(s) occurring in the premises and then conclude by the same rule.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@The case for \nameref{stm_rt_assf} is special.
Here, $S=\texttt{this.$p$ := e}$, and from the premise we know that
\@@amsalign
\par By assumption, $\Delta_{2}(\texttt{this})=\text{var}(I_{2},s_{2})$ and $\Sigma\vdash(I_{2},s_{2})\mathrel{<:}(I_{1},s_{1})$, which implies $s_{2}\sqsubseteq s_{1}$, and therefore also that $s_{2}\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$ by transitivity of $\sqsubseteq$.
Furthermore, by the requirement
\begin{equation*}\forall q\in(\dom\left(\Gamma(I_{1})\right)\cap(\mathcal{N}_{F}\cup\{\texttt{balance}\})).\Gamma(I_{1})(q)=\Gamma(I_{2})(q),\end{equation*}\par\noindent we know that also $\Gamma(I_{2})(p)=\text{var}(B,s^{\prime\prime})$.

\vskip-14.22636pt\end{itemize}\qed\end@proof\par\begin{lemma}If $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{1},\text{env}_{T}\vDash S:\text{cmd}(s)$,
then $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{2},\text{env}_{T}\vDash S:\text{cmd}(s)$.
\end{lemma}\par\@proof Weknowthereexistsatypinginterpretation$$\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$$containing$(S;\bot, \Delta_1, s)$.Wethenconstructanewcandidatetypinginterpretation$$\mathcal{R}$$containing$(S;\bot, \Delta_2, s)$asfollows:\begin{aligned} \mathcal{R}&\triangleq\left\{\,(Q,(\Delta_{2},\hat{\Delta}),s^{\prime})\;\\
\;&~(Q,(\Delta_{1},\hat{\Delta}),s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}\,\right\}\\
&\cup\left\{\,(q_{1};(\text{env}_{V}^{\prime},(\Delta_{2},\hat{\Delta}));q_{2};\bot,\Delta^{\prime},s^{\prime})\;\\
\;&~(q_{1};(\text{env}_{V}^{\prime},(\Delta_{1},\hat{\Delta}));q_{2};\bot,\Delta^{\prime},s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}\,\right\}\par\noindent where$\hat{\Delta}$representsa(possiblyempty)sequenceoffurthertypeassumptionsextending$\Delta_1$resp.{}$\Delta_2$.Clearly,$(S;\bot, \Delta_2, s) \in$\mathcal{R}$$,with$Q = S;\bot$and$\hat{\Delta}= $\emptyset$$.Allthatremainsisnowtoshowthat$$\mathcal{R}$$infactisatypinginterpretation.Forthis,pickanarbitrarytriplet$(Q', \Delta', s') \in$\mathcal{R}$$.Wedistinguish3cases:\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@If $Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{TQ}$ then this satisfies Case~\ref{case:typi1} of Definition~\ref{def:typing_interpretation_stm}.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@Otherwise, if $\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta_{2},\hat{\Delta}$, then we know that $(Q^{\prime},(\Delta_{1},\hat{\Delta}),s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$, which implies that the transition
\begin{equation*}\Sigma;\Gamma;(\Delta_{1},\hat{\Delta})\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}\right>\rightarrow\Sigma;\Gamma;(\Delta_{1},\hat{\Delta}^{\prime})\vDash_{s^{\prime\prime}}\left<Q^{\prime\prime},\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}^{1^{\prime}}\right>\end{equation*}\par\noindent can be concluded by some rule, for any appropriately shaped $\text{env}_{SV}^{1^{\prime}}$.
By Lemma~\ref{lemma:dcall_stm_trans}, a transition
\begin{equation*}\Sigma;\Gamma;(\Delta_{2},\hat{\Delta})\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}\right>\rightarrow\Sigma;\Gamma;(\Delta_{2},\hat{\Delta}^{\prime})\vDash_{s^{\prime\prime}}\left<Q^{\prime\prime},\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}^{2^{\prime}}\right>\end{equation*}\par\noindent can therefore also be concluded, and $(Q^{\prime\prime},(\Delta_{2},\hat{\Delta}),s^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{R}$ by construction.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@Otherwise, if $\Delta^{\prime}\neq\Delta_{2},\hat{\Delta}$, then the triplet must have come from one where $(\text{env}_{V}^{\prime},(\Delta_{1},\hat{\Delta}))$ resided on the stack.
Thus, $Q^{\prime}=q_{1};(\text{env}_{V}^{\prime},(\Delta_{2},\hat{\Delta}));q_{2};\bot$, which by construction must have come from $Q=q_{1};(\text{env}_{V}^{\prime},(\Delta_{1},\hat{\Delta}));q_{2};\bot$.
There are now two sub-cases:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@If $q_{1}$ is non-empty, we know that the transition
\begin{equation*}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta^{\prime}\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<Q,\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}\right>\rightarrow\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta^{\prime\prime}\vDash_{s^{\prime\prime}}\left<Q_{1},\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}^{1}\right>\end{equation*}\par\noindent exists, and we can therefore conclude a similar transition for $Q^{\prime}$, since only the top-most element on the stack can affect transitions.
Thus
\begin{equation*}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta^{\prime}\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}\right>\rightarrow\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta^{\prime\prime}\vDash_{s^{\prime\prime}}\left<Q_{2},\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}^{2}\right>\end{equation*}\par\noindent and $(Q_{2},\Delta^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{R}$ by construction.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@Otherwise, if $q_{1}$ is empty, we have that $Q^{\prime}=(\text{env}_{V}^{\prime},(\Delta_{2},\hat{\Delta}));q_{2};\bot$, which by construction must have come from $Q=(\text{env}_{V}^{\prime},(\Delta_{1},\hat{\Delta}));q_{2};\bot$.
The transition for $Q$ would therefore have been concluded by \nameref{stm_rt_return}, with the reduct triplet $(q_{2};\bot,(\Delta_{1},\hat{\Delta}),s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$.
We can therefore conclude a similar transition for $Q^{\prime}$ by \nameref{stm_rt_return}, and the reduct triplet $(q_{2};\bot,(\Delta_{2},\hat{\Delta}),s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}$ by construction.
\end{itemize}\vskip-14.22636pt\end{itemize}\qed\end@proof\par\par\@proof[Proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:compatibility_stm_dcall}]Bythedefinitionof$\Sigma; \Gamma; \Delta; $\text{env}_{T}$ \vDash$\texttt{dcall $e$.$f$($e_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,e_{h}^{\prime}$)}$ : $\text{cmd}(s)$$,wemustexhibitatypinginterpretation$$\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$$thatcontainsthetriplet$($\texttt{dcall $e$.$f$($e_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,e_{h}^{\prime}$)}$; \text{var}(I^{X}_{s_{1}})&~=\Delta(\texttt{this})\\
\text{proc}(\widetilde{B}_{\widetilde{s}})\texttt{:}s&~=\Gamma(I^{Y})(f)\\
\forall q\in&~(\dom\left(\Gamma(I^{Y})\right)\cap(\mathcal{N}_{F}\cup\{\texttt{balance}\})).\ \Gamma(I^{Y})(q)=\Gamma(I^{X})(q)\par\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{28}}{Semantic type rules for statements (plus rule \nameref{stm_st_fcall} in Fugure~\ref{fig:semantic_type_rules_fallback} for the fallback function)}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[. ]{{Figure 28}}{Semantic type rules for statements (plus rule \nameref{stm_st_fcall} in Fugure~\ref{fig:semantic_type_rules_fallback} for the fallback function)}}}\end{figure}\par\lx@newpage\par\@@numbered@section{appendix}{toc}{Up-to techniques}
\@proof[Proof of Theorem~\ref{lemma:validity_upto_union}]
We proceed by showing that $\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$ is a typing interpretation according to Definition~\ref{def:typing_interpretation_stm}.
\par By assumption, we know that $\mathcal{S}\rightarrowtail\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$, where $\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$ is a typing interpretation.
Now consider an arbitrary triplet $P\in\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$.
If $P\in\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$, then we already know that $P$ satisfies the requirements of Definition~\ref{def:typing_interpretation_stm}, since $\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$ is a typing interpretation.
Otherwise, if $P\in\mathcal{S}$, there are two possibilities:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@If $P~\not\Mapsto$ then $P$ must satisfy Case~\ref{case:prog1} of Definition~\ref{def:progression}.
But then $P$ also satisfies Case~\ref{case:typi1} of Definition~\ref{def:typing_interpretation_stm}, since the requirements are the same.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@If $P\Mapsto P_{1}^{\prime}$ then we know by Case~\ref{case:prog2} of Definition~\ref{def:progression} that, for all $P_{2}^{\prime}$ such that $P\Mapsto P_{2}^{\prime}$ (and, in particular, when $P_{2}^{\prime}=P_{1}^{\prime}$), it holds that $P_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$.
Thus $P_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$ as well.
Therefore $P$ satisfies Case~\ref{case:typi2} of Definition~\ref{def:typing_interpretation_stm}.
\end{itemize}
\par Thus, we conclude that $\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$ indeed is a typing interpretation.
By Definition~\ref{def:typing_interpretation_stm}, it therefore also holds that
\begin{equation*}\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}\subseteq\nu\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}\end{equation*}
since $\nu\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$ is the union of all typing interpretations, and so $\mathcal{S}\subseteq\nu\mathcal{R}_{\Sigma,\Gamma,\text{env}_{T}}$, as required.
\qed\end@proof
\par\par\lx@newpage\par\@@numbered@section{appendix}{toc}{Semantic Typing for Call Integrity}
\par In this appendix, we report the changes needed to the setting of this paper to ensure, through our approach, another security property, specifically designed for smart-contract languages: {call integrity}. This property has been introduced by Grishchenko, Maffei and Schneidewind in \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{Number}{grishchenko2018}{}{}]} to rule out a dangerous phenomenon, called {reentrancy}, in Ethereum smart contracts.
In a nutshell, reentrancy is a pattern based on mutual recursion, where one method $f$ calls another method $g$ while also transferring an amount of currency along with the call; if $g$ then immediately calls $f$ back, it may yield a recursion where $f$ continues to transfer funds to $g$ (for more concrete examples suffering from reentrancy phaenomena, see \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{Number}{AGL25,grishchenko2018}{}{}]}). This could be avoided by using call integrity: this property requires that any call to a method in a `trusted' contract (say, $X$) yields the very same sequence of currency flows (i.e.{} method calls) even if some of the other `untrusted' contracts (or their stored values) are changed.
In a sense, the code and values of the other contracts, which could be controlled by an attacker, must not be able to affect the currency flow from $X$.
\par The main result in \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{Number}{grishchenko2018}{}{}]} is that call integrity implies the absence of reentrancy (see Theorem~1 in {loc.cit.}); thus, call integrity has been identified in the literature as one of the safety properties that smart contracts should have.
In \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{Number}{AGL25}{}{}]} it is proved that the static type system for non-interference (reported in Appendix~\ref{app:syntactic_type_system}) also ensures call integrity for the version of {TinySol}{} \emph{without} fallback functions.
We now want to extend this result to the present version of {TinySol}{}, which includes fallback functions, using the semantic type system we developed in this paper.
\par To this end, we need a few new definitions (we adapt them from \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{Number}{AGL25}{}{}]}; for a more thorough discussion, we refer to the original reference).
Essentially, some modifications are needed to properly define the labelled transitions, since in \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{Number}{AGL25}{}{}]} a big-step operational semantics was adopted, whereas here we use a small-step one.
\par\par\par\par
\par\begin{definition}A \emph{trace} of method invocations is given by
\begin{center}\begin{center}\begin{math}\begin{array}[]{r @{~} c @{~} l @{\qquad} l}\pi&::=&\epsilon~~\big|~~~X\leadsto Y\texttt{.}f\texttt{(}\widetilde{v}\texttt{)}\$z,\pi\end{array}\end{math}\end{center}\end{center}\par\noindent where $X$ is the address of the calling contract, $Y$ is the address of the called contract, $f$ is the method name, and $\widetilde{v}$ and $z$ are the actual parameters.
We annotate the small-step operational semantics with information about the invoked method (if any), to yield labelled transitions of the form $\xrightarrow{\pi}$.
To do this, we first modify the rules in Figures~\ref{fig:semantics_stacks1} and~\ref{fig:semantics_stacks2} as follows:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@in all rules of Figure~\ref{fig:semantics_stacks1}, all occurrences of $\ \rightarrow\ $ in the conclusions of the rules become $\ \xrightarrow{\epsilon}\ $;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@rules \nameref{stm_s_call}, \nameref{stm_s_fcall} and \nameref{stm_s_dcall} respectively become:
\begin{center}\begin{math}\begin{array}[]{l}\dfrac{\text{same premises and side conditions as in rule \nameref{stm_s_call}}}{\left<\texttt{call $e_{1}$.$f$($\widetilde{e}$)\$$e_{2}$};Q,\text{env}_{TSV}\right>\xrightarrow{X\leadsto Y\texttt{.}f\texttt{(}\widetilde{v}\texttt{)}\$z}\left<S;\text{env}_{V};Q,\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}^{\prime}\right>}\\
\dfrac{\text{same premises and side conditions as in rule \nameref{stm_s_fcall}}}{\left<\texttt{call $e_{1}$.$f$($\widetilde{e}$)\$$e_{2}$};Q,\text{env}_{TSV}\right>\xrightarrow{X\leadsto Y\texttt{.}f\texttt{(}\widetilde{v}\texttt{)}\$z}\left<S;\text{env}_{V};Q,\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}^{\prime}\right>}\\
\dfrac{\text{env}_{V}(\texttt{this})=X\qquad\text{same premises and side conditions as in rule \nameref{stm_s_dcall}}}{\left<\texttt{dcall $e$.$f$($\widetilde{e}$)};Q,\text{env}_{TSV}\right>\xrightarrow{X\leadsto Y\texttt{.}f\texttt{(}\widetilde{v}\texttt{)}\$0}\left<S;Q,\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}^{\prime}\right>}\end{array}\end{math}\end{center}\end{itemize}\par\noindent Then, the relation $\xrightarrow{\pi}$ is obtained as the
concatenation of the labelled small-step transitions---that is,
for every $h\geq 0$ and $\pi_{1},\ldots\pi_{h}$ such that every $\pi_{i}$ is either $\epsilon$ or $X_{i}\leadsto Y_{i}\texttt{.}f_{i}\texttt{(}\widetilde{v_{i}}\texttt{)}\$z_{i}$,
we write $\left<Q_{0},\text{env}_{TSV}^{0}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{1},\ldots,\pi_{h}}\left<Q_{h},\text{env}_{TSV}^{h}\right>$ whenever $\left<Q_{0},\text{env}_{TSV}^{0}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\left<Q_{1},\text{env}_{TSV}^{1}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{2}}\ldots\xrightarrow{\pi_{h}}\left<Q_{h},\text{env}_{TSV}^{h}\right>$.
\end{definition}
\par\begin{definition}The \emph{projection} of a trace to a specific contract $X$, written $\pi\downarrow_{X}$, is the trace of calls with $X$ as the calling address.
Formally:
\begin{equation*}\epsilon\downarrow_{X}\ =\ \epsilon\qquad\qquad(Z\leadsto Y\texttt{.}f\texttt{(}\widetilde{v}\texttt{)}\$z,\pi)\downarrow_{X}\ =\ \begin{cases}X\leadsto Y\texttt{.}f\texttt{(}\widetilde{v}\texttt{)}\$z,(\pi\downarrow_{X})&\text{if $Z=X$}\\
\pi\downarrow_{X}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\end{equation*}\end{definition}
\par\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\par Call integrity is formulated in terms of \emph{transactions}, which are essentially sequences of method calls.
These can be rendered in our setting as particular kinds of stacks, consisting of sequences of method calls interspersed with variable environments $\text{env}_{V}$, associating the name of the caller, $X$, to the variable {this}.
By executing such a stack, the variable environment will first be loaded by rule \nameref{untyped_return}, thus setting up the appropriate binding for {this}, and then the method call will be executed by rules \nameref{stm_s_call}/\nameref{stm_s_fcall}, which will use the currently active binding for {this} to set the variable {sender}.
Thus, we formally define transactions as follows:
\begin{center}\begin{center}\begin{math}\begin{array}[]{r @{~} c @{~} l @{\qquad} l}\tau&::=&\bot~~\big|~~~\left\{\,(\texttt{this},X)\,\right\};\texttt{call $Y$.$f$($\widetilde{v}$)\$$z$};\tau\end{array}\end{math}\end{center}\end{center}
\par\noindent Finally, we need another piece of notation: given a (partial) function $\phi$ and a subset $\Xi$ of its domain, we write $\phi{\\
}_{&}~\Xi$ to denote the restriction of $\phi$ to $\Xi$.
\par\begin{definition}Let $\mathcal{N}_{A}$\ be the set of all contracts (addresses),
$\mathcal{X}\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{A}$ denote a set of trusted contracts and
$\mathcal{Y}\triangleq\mathcal{N}_{A}\mathbin{\backslash}\mathcal{X}$ stand for all other contracts.
A contract $X\in\mathcal{X}$ has \emph{call integrity} for $\mathcal{Y}$ if,
for every transaction $\tau$
and environments $\text{env}_{TS}^{1}$ and $\text{env}_{TS}^{2}$ such that
$\text{env}_{TS}^{1}{\\
}_{&}~\mathcal{X}=\text{env}_{TS}^{2}{\\
}_{~}\mathcal{X}$,
$\left<\tau,\text{env}_{TS}^{1},\text{env}_{V}^{\emptyset}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T}^{1},\text{env}_{SV}^{1^{\prime}}\right>$ and
$\left<\tau,\text{env}_{TS}^{2},\text{env}_{V}^{\emptyset}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{2}}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T}^{2},\text{env}_{SV}^{2^{\prime}}\right>$,
it holds that $\pi_{1}\!\downarrow_{X}\ =\pi_{2}\!\downarrow_{X}$.
\end{definition}
\par Notice that, like \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{Number}{AGL25,grishchenko2018}{}{}]}, we require that, to satisfy call integrity, either both execution or none of them must terminate. This was implicit in the definition provided in \cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{Number}{AGL25}{}{}]}, because there a big-step operational semantics was assumed (and so every configuration with an operational evolution was ensured to terminate).
Without this requirement, in a small-step setting there would be no way to guarantee that both traces have the same length.
\par Then, the typed operational semantics remains mostly unchanged; the only modification needed is to remove ``$z\neq 0\Rightarrow$'' from the side conditions of rules \nameref{stm_rt_call} and \nameref{stm_rt_fcall}.
This makes sense because call integrity is concerned with the (sequence of) method invocations, and these are not affected by the fact that the amount of currency transferred is 0 or not.
In contrast, non-interference is concerned with the values contained in some portions of the memories, which may include the {balance} fields; in this case, since with a transferred value $z$ that is 0 such fields are not modified, checking that $s^{\prime}\sqsubseteq s_{3},s_{4}$ is only needed when there is an actual change of balance.
Notice that this change has no effect on the proofs of Lemma~\ref{lemma:compatibility_stm_call} and of the analogous Lemma corresponding to rule \nameref{stm_st_fcall} in Figure~\ref{fig:semantic_type_rules_fallback}.
Hence, also in the new framework tailored for call integrity, the typeability in the static type system implies typeability in the semantic one (see Theorem~\ref{thm:compat_comms}).
\par We can now also prove a result analogous to Theorem~\ref{theorem:stacks_runtime_noninterference}, stating that the typed operational semantics implies call integrity.
To this end, we also need to provide a typed labelled semantics of the form $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<Q,\text{env}_{TSV}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta^{\prime}\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{T},\text{env}_{SV}^{\prime}\right>$, by modifying the rules in Figures~\ref{fig:typed_semantics_stacks1} and~\ref{fig:typed_semantics_stacks2} as we did for the untyped semantics in Definition~\ref{def:traces}.
\par\begin{definition}A contract $X$ is an {$s$-contract w.r.t. $\Gamma$} if $\Gamma(X)=I_{s}$, for some $I$ such that
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$\forall q\in\Gamma(I)\;.\;\Gamma(I)(q)=\text{var}(B_{s})$, and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\forall f\in\Gamma(I)\;.\;\Gamma(I)(f)=\text{proc}(\widetilde{B_{s^{\prime}}})\texttt{:}s$, for some $\widetilde{B_{s^{\prime}}}$.
\end{itemize}\end{definition}
\par\begin{lemma}If $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<S;Q,\text{env}_{TSV}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta^{\prime}\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<Q,\text{env}_{T};\text{env}_{SV}^{\prime}\right>$, then $\pi\downarrow_{X}=\epsilon$, for every $s^{\prime\prime}$-contract $X$ such that $s\not\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$.
\end{lemma}
\@proof By induction on the length of the sequence of labelled transitions (that generate $\pi$).
We have 4 base cases (i.e., when the length is 1):
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@$S=\texttt{skip}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@$S=\texttt{while $e$ do $S^{\prime}$}$, for $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(\text{bool},\hat{s})}\left<e,\text{env}_{SV}\right>\rightarrow\text{F}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@$S=\texttt{$x$ := $e$}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@$S=\texttt{this.$p$ := $e$}$.
\end{enumerate}
In all these cases, $\pi=\epsilon$ and the claim trivially holds.
For the inductive step, we have 5 cases to consider:
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@$S=\texttt{$\text{var}(B,s^{\prime})$ $x$ := $e$ in $S^{\prime}$}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@$S=\texttt{while $e$ do $S^{\prime}$}$, for $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(\text{bool},\hat{s})}\left<e,\text{env}_{SV}\right>\rightarrow\text{T}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@$S=\texttt{if $e$ then $S_{\text{T}}$ else $S_{\text{F}}$}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@$S=\texttt{call $e_{1}$.$m$($\widetilde{e}$)\$$e_{2}$}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@$S=\texttt{dcall $e_{1}$.$m$($\widetilde{e}$)}$.
\end{enumerate}
The first case is easy, since $s^{\prime}=s$ and so can be concluded by induction.
\par In the second and in the third cases, the security level is raised to $\hat{s}$, i.e., the security level needed to evaluate the guard $e$ (viz., $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(\text{bool},\hat{s})}\left<e,\text{env}_{SV}\right>\rightarrow b\in\mathbb{B}$); so, by induction, $\pi\downarrow_{X}=\epsilon$, for every $s^{\prime\prime}$-contract $X$ such that $\hat{s}\not\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$. However, the level can be raised from $s$ to $\hat{s}$ only if $s\sqsubseteq\hat{s}$; so, every $s^{\prime\prime}$ such that $s\not\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$ is also such that $\hat{s}\not\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$. Hence, $\pi\downarrow_{X}=\epsilon$, for every $s^{\prime\prime}$-contract $X$ such that $s\not\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$.
\par For the fourth case, we have that
$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<Q,\text{env}_{TSV}\right>\xrightarrow{X\leadsto Y\texttt{.}f\texttt{(}\widetilde{v}\texttt{)}\$z}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta^{\prime\prime}\vDash_{\hat{s}}\left<S^{\prime};(\text{env}_{V},\Delta);s;Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{T};\text{env}_{SV}^{\prime\prime}\right>$, where $s\sqsubseteq\hat{s}$ and
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$X=\text{env}_{V}(\texttt{this})$ and $\Delta(\texttt{this})=\text{var}(I^{X},s_{1})$, for $s_{1}\sqsubseteq\hat{s}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(I^{Y},\hat{s})}\left<e_{1},\text{env}_{SV}\right>\rightarrow Y$ (for $\Gamma(Y)=(I^{Y},s_{2})$);
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(\text{int},\hat{s})}\left<e_{2},\text{env}_{SV}\right>\rightarrow z$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(\widetilde{B},\widetilde{s}^{\prime})}\left<\widetilde{e},\text{env}_{SV}\right>\rightarrow\widetilde{v}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$f$ is either a method name or $f_{i}=\text{env}_{V}^{i}(\texttt{id})$, in case $m=\texttt{id}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@if $f\not\in\dom\left(\text{env}_{T}(Y)\right)$, then $\text{env}_{T}(Y)(\texttt{fallback})=(\epsilon,S^{\prime})$ and $\Gamma(I^{Y})(\texttt{fallback})=\text{proc}()\texttt{:}\hat{s}$, otherwise $\text{env}_{T}(Y)(f)=(\widetilde{x},S^{\prime})$ and $\Gamma(I^{Y})(f)=\text{proc}(\widetilde{B}_{\widetilde{s}})\texttt{:}\hat{s}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Delta^{\prime\prime}$ is made up by the associations $\texttt{this}:\text{var}(I^{Y},s_{2}),\texttt{sender}:\text{var}(I^{X},s_{1}),\texttt{value}:\text{var}(\text{int},\hat{s})$; moreover, if this is a normal call, it also contains $\widetilde{x}:\widetilde{B}_{\widetilde{s}}$~; otherwise, it also contains the two associations $\texttt{id}:\text{var}(\text{idf},\hat{s}),\texttt{args}:\text{var}(\widetilde{B},\widetilde{s^{\prime}})$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\text{env}_{V}^{\prime\prime}$ is made up by the associations $\texttt{this}:Y,\texttt{sender}:X,\texttt{value}:z$; moreover, if this is a normal call, it also contains $\widetilde{x}:\widetilde{v}$; otherwise, it also contains the two associations $\texttt{id}:f,\texttt{args}:\widetilde{v}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\text{env}_{S}^{\prime\prime}=\text{env}_{S}\kern-2.0pt\left[X\mapsto\text{env}_{F}^{X}[\texttt{balance -= }z]\right]\kern-2.0pt\left[Y\mapsto\text{env}_{F}^{Y}[\texttt{balance += }z]\right]$, where
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$\text{env}_{S}^{i}(X)=\text{env}_{F}^{X}$ and $\text{env}_{S}^{i}(Y)=\text{env}_{F}^{Y}$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Gamma(I^{X})(\texttt{balance})=\text{var}(\text{int},s_{3})$ and $\Gamma(I^{Y})(\texttt{balance})=\text{var}(\text{int},s_{4})$, with $s^{\prime}\sqsubseteq s_{3},s_{4}$.
\end{itemize}\end{itemize}
So, $\pi=X\leadsto Y\texttt{.}f\texttt{(}\widetilde{v}\texttt{)}\$z,\pi^{\prime}$, for some $\pi^{\prime}$. By induction, $\pi^{\prime}\downarrow_{Z}=\epsilon$, for every $s^{\prime\prime}$-contract $Z$ such that $\hat{s}\not\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$ and so, like above, $\pi^{\prime}\downarrow_{Z}=\epsilon$, for every $s^{\prime\prime}$-contract $Z$ such that $s\not\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$, being $s\sqsubseteq\hat{s}$. Concerning $X\leadsto Y\texttt{.}f\texttt{(}\widetilde{v}\texttt{)}\$z$, it is impossible that $X$ is an $s^{\prime\prime}$-contract, for every $s^{\prime\prime}$ such that $s\not\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$: indeed, if this was the case, by the conditions above, we would have that $s^{\prime\prime}=s_{1}\sqsubseteq\hat{s}\sqsubseteq s_{3}=s^{\prime\prime}$, and so $s^{\prime\prime}=\hat{s}$, in contradiction with $s\not\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$ (being $s\sqsubseteq\hat{s}$). Thus, $\pi\downarrow_{Z}=\epsilon$, for every $s^{\prime\prime}$-contract $Z$ such that $s\not\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$, as desired.
\par The fifth case is similar to the previous one. The main difference is the way in which we can conclude that $X$ (the caller) cannot be an $s^{\prime\prime}$-contract, for every $s^{\prime\prime}$ such that $s\not\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$. By contradiction.
First of all, we know that $\Sigma\vdash I^{X}\mathrel{<:}I^{Y}$; this implies that $X$ must have a method $f$ with the same signature as the one in $Y$ but with a possibly {higher} security level (since subtyping is contravariant in the security level of methods). Since $X$ is an $s^{\prime\prime}$-contract, it must be that $\Gamma(I^{X})(f)=\text{proc}(\widetilde{B}_{\widetilde{s}})\texttt{:}s^{\prime\prime}$; being $\Gamma(I^{Y})(f)=\text{proc}(\widetilde{B}_{\widetilde{s}})\texttt{:}\hat{s}$, we have that $\hat{s}\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$. This yields the desired contradiction, since we know that $s\sqsubseteq\hat{s}$, and so also $s\sqsubseteq s^{\prime\prime}$.
\qed\end@proof
\par\begin{theorem}Let $\mathcal{S}\triangleq\left\{\,L,H\,\right\}$ with $L\sqsubseteq L\sqsubseteq H\sqsubseteq H$.
Choose a bipartition of $\mathcal{N}_{A}$\ into $\mathcal{X}$ and $\mathcal{Y}$ and fix a type environment $\Gamma$ with respect to which every $X\in\mathcal{X}$ is a $L$-contract and every $Y\in\mathcal{Y}$ is a $H$-contract.
Also assume that
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vdash\text{env}_{SV}^{i}$ (Well-typedness),
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Gamma\vdash\text{env}_{TSV}^{i}$ (Consistency),
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\text{env}_{SV}^{i};s\vdash Q$ (Well-formedness)
\end{itemize}\par\noindent for $i\in\left\{\,1,2\,\right\}$, and that
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$\text{env}_{TS}^{1}{\\
}_{&}~\mathcal{X}=\text{env}_{TS}^{2}{\\
}_{~}\mathcal{X}$, and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vdash\text{env}_{V}^{1}=_{L}\text{env}_{V}^{2}$.
\end{itemize}\par If
$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<Q,\text{env}_{TSV}^{i}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}\vDash_{s_{i}}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T}^{i},\text{env}_{SV}^{i^{\prime}}\right>$
for $i\in\left\{\,1,2\,\right\}$, then $\pi_{1}\downarrow_{X}=\pi_{2}\downarrow_{X}$, for any $X\in\mathcal{X}$.
\par\end{theorem}
\@proof By induction on the sum of the lengths of the two given sequences of labelled transitions (that generate $\pi_{1}$ and $\pi_{2}$).
The base case (i.e., when the sum is 0) is when $Q=\bot$; in this case, $\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}=\epsilon$ and the claim trivially holds.
For the inductive step, we know that $Q\not\in\mathcal{TQ}$ and we distinguish the top-most element of $Q$.
\par If $Q\in\left\{\,s^{\prime};Q^{\prime}\ ,\ \texttt{del}(x);Q^{\prime}\ ,\ (\text{env}_{V}^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime});Q^{\prime}\,\right\}$, then, for $i\in\{1,2\}$, we have that
$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<Q,\text{env}_{TSV}^{i}\right>\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma;\Gamma;\hat{\Delta}\vDash_{\hat{s}}\left<Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{TS}^{i},\widehat{\text{env}_{V}^{i}}\right>$, where
$$
\hat{\Delta}= \left\{
\begin{array}[]{ll}\Delta'&\mbox{if } Q = ($\text{env}_{V}$',\Delta');Q'\\
\Delta&\mbox{otherwise}\end{array}
\right.
\quad\hat{s} = \left\{
\begin{array}[]{ll}s'&\mbox{if } Q = s';Q'\\
s&\mbox{otherwise}\end{array}
\right.
\quad\widehat{$\text{env}_{V}$^i} = \left\{
\begin{array}[]{ll}$\text{env}_{V}$'&\mbox{if } Q = ($\text{env}_{V}$',\Delta');Q'\\
$\text{env}_{V}$^i&\mbox{otherwise}\end{array}
\right.
$$
We can thus immediately conclude by induction applied to $\Sigma;\Gamma;\hat{\Delta}\vDash_{\hat{s}}\left<Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{TS}^{i},\widehat{\text{env}_{V}^{i}}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}\vDash_{s_{i}}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T}^{i},\text{env}_{SV}^{i^{\prime}}\right>$, for $i\in\{1,2\}$.
\par The delicate case is when $Q=S;Q^{\prime}$, for some $S$ and $Q^{\prime}$.
If $s=H$, then the claim immediately holds by Lemma~\ref{lem:HvsL}.
So let us assume that $s=L$ and reason by case analysis on $S$.
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$S=\texttt{skip}$: in this case, $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<Q,\text{env}_{TSV}^{i}\right>\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{TSV}^{i}\right>$, for $i\in\{1,2\}$, and we trivially conclude by induction.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$S=\texttt{$\text{var}(B,s^{\prime})$ $x$ := $e$ in $S^{\prime}$}$: in this case, $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<Q,\text{env}_{TSV}^{i}\right>\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta,x:\text{var}(B,s^{\prime})\vDash_{s}\left<S^{\prime};\text{del}(x);Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{TS}^{i};\text{env}_{V}^{i},x:v_{i}\right>$, where
$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(B,s^{\prime})}\left<e,\text{env}_{SV}^{i}\right>\rightarrow v_{i}$, for $i\in\{1,2\}$.
If $s^{\prime}=L$, then $\Gamma;\Delta\vdash\text{env}_{S}^{1}=_{L}\text{env}_{S}^{2}$, since we assume that $\text{env}_{S}^{1}{\\
}_{&}~\mathcal{X}=\text{env}_{S}^{2}{\\
}_{~}\mathcal{X}$ and all elements of every $X\in\mathcal{X}$ are labelled $L$.
Moreover, we assume that $\Gamma;\Delta\vdash\text{env}_{V}^{1}=_{L}\text{env}_{V}^{2}$.
Thus, by Corollary~\ref{corollary:expressions_runtime_noninterference}, we obtain that $v_{1}=v_{2}=v$, and so
\begin{equation*}\Gamma;\Delta,x:\text{var}(B_{L})\vdash\text{env}_{V}^{1},x:v=_{L}\text{env}_{V}^{2},x:v.\end{equation*}\par\noindent Otherwise, $s^{\prime}=H$ and so $x$ is an $H$-variable; hence,
\begin{equation*}\Gamma;\Delta,x:\text{var}(B_{H})\vdash\text{env}_{V}^{1},x:v_{1}=_{L}\text{env}_{V}^{2},x:v_{2}.\end{equation*}\par\noindent In both cases, we can apply the induction hypothesis to $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta,x:\text{var}(B,s^{\prime})\vDash_{s}\left<S^{\prime};\text{del}(x);Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{TS}^{i};\text{env}_{V}^{i},x:v_{i}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}\vDash_{s_{i}}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T}^{i},\text{env}_{SV}^{i^{\prime}}\right>$, for $i\in\{1,2\}$, and conclude.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@The cases for $S=\texttt{$x$ := $e$}$ and $S=\texttt{this.$p$ := $e$}$ are similar to the previous one (just notice that here $\Delta$ is not changed in the first transition).
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$S=\texttt{if $e$ then $S_{\text{T}}$ else $S_{\text{F}}$}$: in this case, we have that
$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<Q,\text{env}_{TSV}^{i}\right>\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<S_{b_{i}};s;Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{TSV}^{i}\right>$, for $i\in\{1,2\}$, where
$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(\text{bool},s^{\prime})}\left<e,\text{env}_{SV}^{i}\right>\rightarrow b_{i}(\in\mathbb{B})$ and $s\sqsubseteq s^{\prime}$.
We distinguish two sub-cases:
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@$s^{\prime}=s(=L)$:
since we assume that $\text{env}_{S}^{1}{\\
}_{&}~\mathcal{X}=\text{env}_{S}^{2}{\\
}_{~}\mathcal{X}$ and $\Gamma;\Delta\vdash\text{env}_{V}^{1}=_{L}\text{env}_{V}^{2}$, by Corollary~\ref{corollary:expressions_runtime_noninterference}, we obtain that $b_{1}=b_{2}=b\in\mathbb{B}$.
So, we can apply the induction
hypothesis to $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<S_{b};s;Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{TSV}^{i}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}\vDash_{s_{i}}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T}^{i},\text{env}_{SV}^{i^{\prime}}\right>$, for $i\in\{1,2\}$, and conclude.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@$s^{\prime}=H$: if $S_{b_{1}}=S_{b_{2}}$, we reason like in the sub-case above. Otherwise, we have that each $\pi_{i}$ is equal to $\pi_{i}^{\prime},\pi_{i}^{\prime\prime}$, where
$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<S_{b_{i}};s;Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{TSV}^{i}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}^{\prime}}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{TSV}^{i^{\prime\prime}}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}\vDash_{s_{i}}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T}^{i},\text{env}_{SV}^{i^{\prime}}\right>$.
Being $s^{\prime}=H$, by Lemma~\ref{lem:HvsL} we have that $\pi_{1}^{\prime}\downarrow_{X}=\pi_{2}^{\prime}\downarrow_{X}=\epsilon$, for any $X\in\mathcal{X}$; then, by induction hypothesis, we obtain that $\pi_{1}^{\prime\prime}\downarrow_{X}=\pi_{2}^{\prime\prime}\downarrow_{X}$, that allows us to conclude.
\end{enumerate}\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$S=\texttt{while $e$ do $S^{\prime}$}$: this case is similar to the previous one. If $e$ evaluates to the same boolean value in the two environments,
we simply apply the induction. Otherwise, $s^{\prime}=H$ and the computation where $e$ evaluates to $\text{F}$ yields $Q^{\prime}$ by exhibiting trace $\epsilon$, whereas the the computation where $e$ evaluates to $\text{T}$ will yield $Q^{\prime}$ after some trace $\pi$. However, since the overall level has been raised to $H$, because of Lemma~\ref{lem:HvsL}, we have that $\pi\downarrow_{X}=\epsilon$, for any $X\in\mathcal{X}$, and we conclude by induction.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$S=\texttt{call $e_{1}$.$m$($\widetilde{e}$)\$$e_{2}$}$: in this case, we have that
$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<Q,\text{env}_{TSV}^{i}\right>\xrightarrow{X_{i}\leadsto Y_{i}\texttt{.}f_{i}\texttt{(}\widetilde{v}_{i}\texttt{)}\$z_{i}}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}^{\prime}\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<S_{i};(\text{env}_{V}^{i},\Delta);s;Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{T}^{i};\text{env}_{SV}^{i^{\prime\prime}}\right>$, for $i\in\{1,2\}$, where $s\sqsubseteq s^{\prime}$ and
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$X_{i}=\text{env}_{V}^{i}(\texttt{this})$ and $\Delta(\texttt{this})=\text{var}(I^{X},s_{1})$, for $s_{1}\sqsubseteq s^{\prime}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(I^{Y_{i}},s^{\prime})}\left<e_{1},\text{env}_{SV}^{i}\right>\rightarrow Y_{i}$ (for $\Gamma(Y_{i})=(I^{Y_{i}},s_{2}^{i})$);
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(\text{int},s^{\prime})}\left<e_{2},\text{env}_{SV}^{i}\right>\rightarrow z_{i}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(\widetilde{B},\widetilde{s}^{\prime})}\left<\widetilde{e},\text{env}_{SV}^{i}\right>\rightarrow\widetilde{v}_{i}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$f_{i}$ is either a method name $m$ for both $i$'s, or $f_{i}=\text{env}_{V}^{i}(\texttt{id})$, in case $m=\texttt{id}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@if $f_{i}\not\in\dom\left(\text{env}_{T}^{i}(Y_{i})\right)$, then $\text{env}_{T}^{i}(Y_{i})(\texttt{fallback})=(\epsilon,S_{i})$ and $\Gamma(I^{Y_{i}})(\texttt{fallback})=\text{proc}()\texttt{:}s^{\prime}$, otherwise $\text{env}_{T}^{i}(Y_{i})(f_{i})=(\widetilde{x},S_{i})$ and $\Gamma(I^{Y_{i}})(f_{i})=\text{proc}(\widetilde{B}_{\widetilde{s}})\texttt{:}s^{\prime}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Delta^{\prime}_{i}$ is made up by the associations $\texttt{this}:\text{var}(I^{Y_{i}},s_{2}^{i}),\texttt{sender}:\text{var}(I^{X},s_{1}),\texttt{value}:\text{var}(\text{int},s^{\prime})$; moreover, if in environments $i$ this is a normal call, it also contains $\widetilde{x}:\widetilde{B}_{\widetilde{s}}$~; otherwise, it also contains the two associations $\texttt{id}:\text{var}(\text{idf},s^{\prime}),\texttt{args}:\text{var}(\widetilde{B},\widetilde{s}^{\prime})$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\text{env}_{V}^{i^{\prime\prime}}$ is made up by the associations $\texttt{this}:Y_{i},\texttt{sender}:X_{i},\texttt{value}:z_{i}$; moreover, if in environments $i$ this is a normal call, it also contains $\widetilde{x}:\widetilde{v}_{i}$; otherwise, it also contains the two associations $\texttt{id}:f_{i},\texttt{args}:\widetilde{v}_{i}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\text{env}_{S}^{i^{\prime\prime}}=\text{env}_{S}\kern-2.0pt\left[X_{i}\mapsto\text{env}_{F}^{X_{i}}[\texttt{balance -= }z_{i}]\right]\kern-2.0pt\left[Y_{i}\mapsto\text{env}_{F}^{Y_{i}}[\texttt{balance += }z_{i}]\right]$, where
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$\text{env}_{S}^{i}(X_{i})=\text{env}_{F}^{X_{i}}$ and $\text{env}_{S}^{i}(Y_{i})=\text{env}_{F}^{Y_{i}}$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Gamma(I^{X})(\texttt{balance})=\text{var}(\text{int},s_{3})$ and $\Gamma(I^{Y_{i}})(\texttt{balance})=\text{var}(\text{int},s_{4}^{i})$, with $s^{\prime}\sqsubseteq s_{3},s_{4}^{i}$.
\end{itemize}\end{itemize}\par If $s^{\prime}=L$, then like above, by Corollary~\ref{corollary:expressions_runtime_noninterference}, we obtain that
$X_{1}=X_{2}$, $Y_{1}=Y_{2}$, $f_{1}=f_{2}$, $\widetilde{v}_{1}=\widetilde{v}_{2}$, and $z_{1}=z_{2}$; so, consequently, also that $\Delta_{1}^{\prime}=\Delta_{2}^{\prime}$, $S_{1}=S_{2}$, $\text{env}_{S}^{1^{\prime\prime}}=\text{env}_{S}^{2^{\prime\prime}}$ and $\text{env}_{V}^{1^{\prime\prime}}=\text{env}_{V}^{2^{\prime\prime}}$. Thus, by induction, we can easily conclude.
\par If $s^{\prime}=H$, then we reason as follows. First, observe that the two $\pi_{i}$'s are equal to $X_{i}\leadsto Y_{i}\texttt{.}f_{i}\texttt{(}\widetilde{v}_{i}\texttt{)}\$z_{i},\pi_{i}^{\prime},\pi_{i}^{\prime\prime}$, where
$\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}^{\prime}\vDash_{s^{\prime}}\left<S_{i};(\text{env}_{V}^{i},\Delta);s;Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{T}^{i};\text{env}_{SV}^{i^{\prime\prime}}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}^{\prime}}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{s}\left<Q^{\prime},\text{env}_{TV}^{i};\text{env}_{S}^{i^{\prime\prime\prime}}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}\vDash_{s}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T}^{i};\text{env}_{SV}^{i^{\prime}}\right>$.
For any $X\in\mathcal{X}$, we have that
$\pi_{1}^{\prime}\downarrow_{X}=\pi_{2}^{\prime}\downarrow_{X}=\epsilon$ (by Lemma~\ref{lem:HvsL}) and
$\pi_{1}^{\prime\prime}\downarrow_{X}=\pi_{2}^{\prime\prime}\downarrow_{X}$ (by induction).
We are left with the first label of the traces.
The crucial observation is that $X_{1}$ and $X_{2}$ must belong to $\mathcal{Y}$, because their {balance} fields are $H$ (given that $H=s^{\prime}\sqsubseteq s_{3}$); hence, the first labels of the two traces are excluded from any projection on a contract from $\mathcal{X}$ and we conclude.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$S=\texttt{dcall $e_{1}$.$m$($\widetilde{e}$)}$: this case is similar to the previous one when no fallback function is invoked.
The only notable difference is that in this case we have no explicit typing information about the {balance} fields, since they are not modified by the method invocation. However, we know that $\Gamma(I^{Y_{i}})(\texttt{balance})=\Gamma(I^{X})(\texttt{balance})$. In the case when $s^{\prime}=H$, this again entails that $X_{1},X_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}$, because $Y_{1},Y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}$, being that $\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta\vDash_{(I^{Y_{i}},s^{\prime})}\left<e_{1},\text{env}_{SV}^{i}\right>\rightarrow Y_{i}$.
\vskip-14.22636pt\end{itemize}
\qed\end@proof
\par\begin{corollary}Assume the same security labelling as in Theorem~\ref{theorem:stacks_runtime_CI} and that
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\text{env}_{S}^{i}$ (Well-typedness),
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Gamma\vdash\text{env}_{TS}^{i};\text{env}_{V}^{\emptyset}$ (Consistency),
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\text{env}_{S}^{i};\text{env}_{V}^{\emptyset};s\vdash\tau$ (Well-formedness),
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\text{env}_{T}^{i}\vDash\text{env}_{T}^{i}$ ($\text{env}_{T}$ type safety),
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\Sigma;\Gamma;\emptyset;\text{env}_{T}^{i}\vDash\tau:\text{cmd}(s)$ (stack type safety),
\end{itemize}\par\noindent for $i\in\left\{\,1,2\,\right\}$, and $\text{env}_{TS}^{1}{\\
}_{&}~\mathcal{X}=\text{env}_{TS}^{2}{\\
}_{~}\mathcal{X}$.
\par If
$\left<\tau,\text{env}_{TS}^{i},\text{env}_{V}^{\emptyset}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T}^{i},\text{env}_{SV}^{i^{\prime}}\right>$
for $i\in\left\{\,1,2\,\right\}$, then
$\pi_{1}\downarrow_{X}=\pi_{2}\downarrow_{X}$, for any $X\in\mathcal{X}$.
\end{corollary}
\@proof The crucial observation is that, if $\Sigma;\Gamma;\emptyset;\text{env}_{T}^{i}\vDash\tau:\text{cmd}(s)$ and
$\left<\tau,\text{env}_{TS}^{i},\text{env}_{V}^{\emptyset}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T}^{i},\text{env}_{SV}^{i^{\prime}}\right>$,
then $\Sigma;\Gamma;\emptyset\vDash_{s}\left<\tau,\text{env}_{TS}^{i},\text{env}_{V}^{\emptyset}\right>\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}}\Sigma;\Gamma;\Delta_{i}\vDash_{s_{i}}\left<\bot,\text{env}_{T}^{i},\text{env}_{SV}^{i^{\prime}}\right>$; this holds otherwise we would eventually have a non-terminal stack that has no outgoing typed transitions (in contradiction with the type safe assumption).
\qed\end@proof
\par To conclude, a challenging avenue to explore is to to drop the condition that both executions have to terminate in Definition~\ref{def:call_integrity} and instead show that if neither of the two executions get stuck (i.e.~if there were no type errors), then we cannot find in finitely many steps a point at which the two call-traces disagree.
This would extend the notion of call-integrity provided in~\cite[citep]{[\@@bibref{Number}{grishchenko2018}{}{}]} to non-terminating programs.

The
\Delta_{i}
and
s_{i}
may differ because of subtyping of the method signature, and because of different types for the magic variable
this
. Each of the types in any
\Delta_{i}
may be supertypes of the types from the signature of
f
on
I^{Y}
, and
s_{i}\sqsupseteq s
, by contravariance of the type constructor.

There is actually a minor, technical difference: Subject reduction cannot be shown directly, using the untyped semantics, because it does not store information about entering a heightened security context, which we, in the typed semantics, represented by updating the security level on the turnstile
\vDash_{s^{\prime}}
and pushing the original security level
s
onto the stack. For example, in the case of
if-else
, execution of the guarded statement
S_{b}
must preserve the judgment
\text{cmd}(s^{\prime})
, and not merely
\text{cmd}(s)
for all its execution steps, but the untyped semantics does not take this difference into account.
